## Task 1.14: `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` becomes a thin adapter — and the array is deleted

**The class name, namespace and public static signatures are PRESERVED.** W-LOT-A-1a never states a preservation rule for the name; **this design states it**, so a later lane cannot rename the class and quietly break the staging protocol.

**The full dependency census — SEVEN PHP references, not five**, each disposed of by name:

| Reference | Disposition |
|---|---|
| `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ExportFrontendPermissionsMap.php:29-30` | **re-pointed** at the registry (Task 1.17) |
| `apps/api/database/seeders/DatabaseSeeder.php:76` | verified: `$this->call(RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::class)` still works through the shim |
| `apps/api/database/seeders/DemoTenantSeeder.php:111` | verified |
| `apps/api/database/seeders/CoffeeShopSeeder.php:116` | verified |
| `apps/api/database/seeders/ParapharmacySeeder.php:316` | verified |
| `apps/api/database/seeders/ProductionSeeder.php:70` | verified |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantInitializationService.php:213-216` | **DELETED, not re-pointed** — the provisioning trigger became a direct `PermissionSyncService::sync()` call in Task 1.11 |

Plus three non-PHP: the entrypoint `--class` string (`dev:apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:165` — **removed with the block** in Task 1.11), two staging `docker exec` heredocs in the lane's evidence protocol, and the lane's mandatory PG preservation test named after the class.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Database\Seeders;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services\PermissionSyncService;
use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
use Illuminate\Database\Seeder;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Log;
use Spatie\Permission\PermissionRegistrar;

/**
 * THIN ADAPTER. The 871-line array is gone; PermissionRegistry owns the
 * catalogue and PermissionSyncService owns the write.
 *
 * The class NAME, NAMESPACE and the two public static SIGNATURES are preserved
 * deliberately: the exporter, five seeders and lane/w-lot-a-1a's five-push
 * staging protocol all name them. W-LOT-A-1a never stated that preservation
 * rule; this design does.
 */
final class RolesAndPermissionsSeeder extends Seeder
{
    public function __construct(
        private readonly PermissionSyncService $sync,
        private readonly PermissionRegistrar $permissionRegistrar,
    ) {}

    /**
     * Q-w1-3: the lane's MARKED-TENANT PRESERVATION BRANCH is kept VERBATIM.
     * On a marked tenant with LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE off, this emits the
     * WLOTA1A-RESEED marker and RETURNS WITHOUT DELEGATING, so that lane's
     * mandatory PG test (RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest, which asserts
     * the permission snapshot is BYTE-IDENTICAL after a flag-off reseed) keeps
     * passing. Every other path delegates.
     *
     * The lane's ENFORCEMENT branch, which invoked LotActionPermissionDelta, is
     * DELETED: nothing in wave 1 calls the delta, and
     * PermissionSyncServiceTest::sync_references_no_lot_action_symbol asserts it.
     */
    public function run(): void
    {
        $tenantId = $this->currentTenantId();
        if ($tenantId === null) {
            return;
        }

        if (! (bool) config('lot_action_permissions.enforce')
            && $this->markedTenantCarriesWlota1aDelta($tenantId)) {
            $marker = sprintf(
                'WLOTA1A-RESEED tenant=%s mode=preserve outcome=SKIPPED reason=marked_tenant_delta_preserved',
                $tenantId,
            );
            $this->command?->info($marker);
            Log::channel('stderr')->info($marker);

            return;
        }

        $result = $this->sync->sync($tenantId, write: true);
        $marker = $result->markerLine($tenantId, true);
        $this->command?->info($marker);
        Log::channel('stderr')->info($marker);
    }

    /**
     * @internal Compatibility shim — call PermissionRegistry directly in new code.
     *           `app()` is the ONE sanctioned exception to rule 13 in this wave:
     *           the method is static, so there is no constructor to inject into,
     *           and it exists solely to keep the exporter's and the lane's
     *           contracts intact. Wave 3 migrates the exporter and deletes both.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    public static function permissionNames(): array
    {
        return app(PermissionRegistry::class)->keys();
    }

    /**
     * @internal Compatibility shim — see permissionNames().
     *
     * @return array<string, list<string>>
     */
    public static function rolePermissionGrants(): array
    {
        return app(PermissionRegistry::class)->templateGrants();
    }

    private function currentTenantId(): ?string
    {
        $teamId = $this->permissionRegistrar->getPermissionsTeamId();

        return is_string($teamId) && $teamId !== '' ? $teamId : null;
    }

    /** The lane's predicate, kept here verbatim for the preservation branch above. */
    private function markedTenantCarriesWlota1aDelta(string $tenantId): bool
    {
        return app(\App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Repositories\RoleMarkerRepository::class)
            ->tenantCarriesMarker('w-lot-a-1a');
    }
}
~~~

> **Three `app()` calls, and all three are inside the sanctioned exception**: two `public static` shims with no constructor, and `markedTenantCarriesWlota1aDelta()`, which the preservation branch calls from `run()` and which **could** be constructor-injected. **Inject it.** Add `private readonly RoleMarkerRepository $roleMarkers` to the constructor and call `$this->roleMarkers->tenantCarriesMarker('w-lot-a-1a')`; the `app()` form above is shown only to make the seam visible. The PHPStan `app()`-ban allow-list gains **exactly two** entries: `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::permissionNames` and `::rolePermissionGrants`.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — delete the 871-line array in the SAME commit that the manifests become authoritative,** and run `PermissionCatalogueParityTest` immediately. It exists exactly to make this cut-over reviewable.
- [ ] **Step 2 — re-point `LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest` at its committed fixture IN THIS COMMIT.** Before it, the assertion is method-versus-class; after it, fixture-versus-class; **it is never a hand copy of an 800-line array.** Leaving it pointed at the shim would turn it into fixture-versus-registry and stop it proving what it was written to prove (this is wave 0b's residual **R-0b-6**).
- [ ] **Step 3 — delete `permissions:dump-baseline`** (`apps/api/app/Console/Commands/DumpLegacyRoleBaseline.php`) — the fixture it produced is now frozen and the lane method it read is a shim.
- [ ] **Step 4 — verify each of the seven references**, by running the seeder or command that reaches it, and record the seven verdicts in the handback. **"It compiles" is not a verdict.** The seven commands:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
      php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map --path=/tmp/w1-map-check.ts && head -3 /tmp/w1-map-check.ts     # 1
      php artisan db:seed --class='Database\Seeders\DatabaseSeeder' --force                                       # 2
      php artisan db:seed --class='Database\Seeders\DemoTenantSeeder' --force                                     # 3
      php artisan db:seed --class='Database\Seeders\CoffeeShopSeeder' --force                                     # 4
      php artisan db:seed --class='Database\Seeders\ParapharmacySeeder' --force                                   # 5
      php artisan db:seed --class='Database\Seeders\ProductionSeeder' --force                                     # 6
      grep -n 'RolesAndPermissionsSeeder\|Artisan' app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantInitializationService.php   # 7 — expect NO output
      ```
- [ ] **Step 5 — run the lane's own preservation test**, which is the only mechanical proof Q-w1-3 was honoured:
      ```
      DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w1 DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w1_central \
        php artisan test -c phpunit-pgsql.xml tests/Feature/Identity/RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest.php \
                                              tests/Feature/Identity/LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest.php \
                                              tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionCatalogueParityTest.php
      ```
- [ ] **Step 6 — stage and commit:**
      ```
      git rm apps/api/app/Console/Commands/DumpLegacyRoleBaseline.php
      git add apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest.php \
              apps/api/phpstan-baseline.neon
      ```
      Commit message, in full:
      ```
      Phase 1.14: turn RolesAndPermissionsSeeder into a thin adapter and delete the 871-line array

      The class name, namespace and two public static signatures are PRESERVED —
      the exporter, five seeders and lane/w-lot-a-1a's staging protocol all name
      them, and this design states the preservation rule that lane never did. Seven
      PHP references are disposed of individually; the seventh,
      TenantInitializationService's, was DELETED rather than re-pointed in Phase 1.11
      because the provisioning trigger is now a direct PermissionSyncService::sync()
      call.

      run() KEEPS the lane's marked-tenant preservation branch verbatim (Q-w1-3): a
      flag-off reseed on a marked tenant emits WLOTA1A-RESEED and returns without
      delegating, so RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest — that lane's mandatory
      PG evidence — stays green. A bare delegation would have reddened it, because the

      LegacyRoleBaselineParityTest is re-pointed at its committed fixture IN THIS
      ```

## Task 1.15: `permissions:scaffold` — the deterministic generator behind contract AI-1

**The honest contract, restated.** Rev 1 of the spec claimed that adding a `PermissionDefinition` *"and nothing else"* produced enum reachability and en/fr/ar labels. **It cannot:** the per-module enums are separately authored classes, the locale files are separate human-edited artifacts, and a brand-new module also needs its manifest tagged in its service provider. A test can **refuse** those omissions; it cannot synthesise them. The contract is therefore **one definition plus one generator run**.

**Five artifacts, not four** (rev 1 said "four" and then listed five). They are: the **manifest entry**, the **enum case**, and **three** locale stubs — `en`, `fr`, `ar`. `permissions:scaffold` writes all five deterministically, and it **refuses** rather than guessing when the module has no manifest or no enum yet, printing the two files to create and the tag line to add.

### The determinism contract, stated as five mechanical rules

| # | Rule | Why it is not optional |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | **The manifest entry is inserted at a FIXED ANCHOR** — the line matching `/^\s*return \[$/` inside `definitions()` — and the resulting list is **re-sorted by `(resource, action)`**, then re-emitted. The command never appends at the cursor and never appends at the end. | A generator whose output depends on where the previous one stopped is not a generator. Sorting means two invocations in either order produce the same file. |
| 2 | **The enum case is appended immediately before the class's closing brace**, i.e. before the LAST `/^\}$/` line of the file, then the case block is re-sorted by case name. | Same reason; and appending before the closing brace is the only insertion point that is well-defined for an enum with no methods. |
| 3 | **JSON is encoded with `JSON_PRETTY_PRINT \| JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES \| JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE`**, keys sorted with `ksort($block, SORT_STRING)`, and the file ends with exactly one `\n`. | `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE` is required or the Arabic bundle is rewritten into `\uXXXX` escapes on every run and the diff is unreadable; `SORT_STRING` because PHP's default sort is locale-sensitive. |
| 4 | **Every write is atomic**: render to `<path>.scaffold.tmp` in the same directory, `fflush`, then `rename()`. A partial write leaves the original untouched. | A generator interrupted halfway must not corrupt a 300-key locale bundle. |
| 5 | **The command is IDEMPOTENT**: a second invocation with the same arguments detects the key already present in the manifest and exits `0` having written **nothing** (byte-identical mtime-independent check via a content hash before and after). | This is what `ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest`'s two-clean-worktree case actually measures. |

**A brand-new module's provider tag is NOT generated.** The command detects a missing manifest class and **refuses** with the exact three things to do — create `app/Modules/<M>/Domain/Authorization/<M>PermissionManifest.php`, create `app/Modules/<M>/Domain/Enums/<M>Permission.php`, add `$this->app->tag([...], 'permission.manifests');` to `<M>`'s provider — because writing a new class into an unknown provider layout is exactly the kind of guess that produces a file nobody reviews.

**Files:** `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest.php`.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the signature:**
      ```
      permissions:scaffold
          {module   : the owning module, e.g. Procurement}
          {resource : kebab-case resource, e.g. purchase-orders}
          {verb     : a PermissionVerb case value, e.g. approve}
          {--qualifier=  : snake_case narrowing}
          {--template=*  : template keys receiving it by default (admin is implicit and refused)}
          {--sod-group=}
          {--dry-run     : print the five diffs; write nothing}
      ```
- [ ] **Step 2 — the command**, implementing rules 1–5 above. Its `handle()` is, in order:
      1. resolve the verb through `PermissionVerb::tryFrom()`; on failure, error listing **the closed set**, exit 1;
      2. construct the definition through `PermissionDefinition::make()` so a malformed resource or qualifier throws **the same message `make()` throws** — the command never re-implements the validation;
      3. refuse `admin` in `--template` with `make()`'s own message;
      4. locate `app/Modules/<module>/Domain/Authorization/<module>PermissionManifest.php` and `…/Domain/Enums/<module>Permission.php`; if either is missing, print the three-step refusal above and exit 1;
      5. if the key is already in the manifest, print `already declared` and exit **0 without writing** (rule 5);
      6. render the five artifacts, each through the atomic writer (rule 4), unless `--dry-run`, which prints a unified diff per artifact instead;
      7. print the five paths and remind the operator to run `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map`.
- [ ] **Step 3 — `ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest`**, six named cases:
      - **`two_clean_worktrees_produce_byte_identical_output`** — copy the repo's `app/Modules/<M>` and the three locale files into two temp trees, run the identical invocation in each, `assertSame(file_get_contents(a), file_get_contents(b))` for all five artifacts. This is the case that makes determinism checkable;
      - `a_second_run_writes_nothing` — content hash of all five files before and after (rule 5);
      - `insertion_is_sorted_not_appended` — scaffold a key that sorts *first* and assert it lands first, not last (rule 1);
      - `admin_in_template_is_refused` with `make()`'s message;
      - `a_verb_outside_the_enum_is_refused` and the message **lists the closed set**;
      - `a_missing_manifest_refuses_with_the_three_steps` — the brand-new-module path, asserting all three instructions appear.
- [ ] **Step 4 — run, analyse, format, stage, commit:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
      php artisan test tests/Feature/Authorization/ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest.php
      ./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php
      ./vendor/bin/pint --test app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php tests/Feature/Authorization/ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest.php
      git add apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest.php
      ```
      Subject: **`Phase 1.15: add permissions:scaffold, the deterministic five-artifact generator behind contract AI-1`**, both trailers.

## Task 1.16: the static guards

### 16a — `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` (PHPStan)

Flags **any** dotted lowercase string literal matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]*)+$` when it appears as an argument to `->can()`, `->cant()`, `->hasPermissionTo()`, `->hasAnyPermission()`, `Gate::authorize()`, `Gate::allows()`, `Gate::denies()`, or inside a `->middleware([...])` / `middleware('can:…')` / `middleware('require.any.permission:…')` string.

**Two error classes, and the second is the important one.** Flagging only literals *already in* the registry lets the single most damaging mistake through: a typo such as `invoices.posst` is absent from the registry and would therefore **pass** — while at runtime Spatie swallows `PermissionDoesNotExist`, so the route **silently 403s for everyone including `admin`**.

| Literal | Verdict | Message |
|---|---|---|
| in the registry | **error**, use the enum | *"permission literal 'purchase-orders.confirm' — use `ProcurementPermission::PurchaseOrdersConfirm->value`."* |
| **not** in the registry | **error**, unknown key | *"unknown permission literal 'invoices.posst' — it is in no PermissionManifest, so this gate denies every caller including admin. Declare it, or fix the typo."* |

**Restricting the shape match to authorization CALL SITES** (rather than to any dotted string anywhere) is what keeps the rule free of false positives on config and i18n keys, so the registry lookup is no longer load-bearing for precision.

**The rule reads the registry WITHOUT booting the application.** PHPStan runs in its own process and a rule that calls `app()` inside `processNode()` is a rule that boots Laravel per file. Instead the rule takes a **generated key list** — `apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json`, written by `permissions:export-frontend-map` in Task 1.17 and regenerated by the preflight drift guard — injected through the PHPStan service definition:
```neon
services:
    -
        class: App\PHPStan\Rules\ForbidPermissionStringLiteral
        arguments:
            keyFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json
            baselineFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json
        tags:
            - phpstan.rules.rule
```
If the key file is missing the rule reports **one** error on the first file it sees — *"permission-registry-keys.json is missing; run `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map`"* — rather than silently passing every literal.

**Allowed call sites, encoded in the rule:** `app/Modules/*/Domain/Authorization/*PermissionManifest.php`, `app/Modules/*/Domain/Enums/*Permission.php`, `app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/*`, `database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php` (the shim), `app/Console/Commands/SyncPermissions.php`, `app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php`, `app/Console/Commands/ReportDeadPermissions.php`, and everything under `tests/`.

**The ignore list is large, and that is the honest cost.** The baseline is **generated, never hand-counted**:
```
cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 --error-format=json app > /tmp/w1-literals.json
php -r '…extract this rule identifier'"'"'s messages, sort, write permission-literal-baseline.json…'
```
Record the two counts (route-middleware strings; call sites) **as measured**, not as rev 1's remembered `608 / 309`. What the baseline buys is that the **door closes behind** the existing sites: a *new* literal is an error on day one, and the list only shrinks (wave 3 empties it).

### 16b — `RoutePermissionLiteralTest` (AST, with constant-expression evaluation)

**PHPStan analyses `app/` only** (`dev:apps/api/phpstan.neon:5-8`), so the rule **does** reach module route files under `app/Modules/*/Presentation/` — it is only the **top-level `routes/` directory** that is outside its paths. Rev 1's *"PHPStan cannot see route files"* was overbroad; the sentence after it had the distinction right (plan gate r1, minor). Widening PHPStan's paths would pull unrelated files into level 8 and need its own baseline, so a companion architecture test parses **every `app/Modules/*/Presentation/routes*.php` and every file under `routes/`** with `nikic/php-parser` (already a PHPStan dependency, vendored at `apps/api/vendor/nikic/php-parser`), extracts each `can:` / `require.any.permission:` argument, and asserts every extracted key is in `activeKeys()`.

**It STATICALLY EVALUATES constant expressions and ACCEPTS them.** A guard that rejects the migration target is not a guard, it is a wall — and `'can:'.InventoryPermission::TransfersReconcile->value` is exactly the form this design prescribes:

| Expression in a route file | Handling |
|---|---|
| `'can:inventory.transfers.reconcile'` | plain `String_` — resolved, reported as a **literal** (use the enum) **and** checked against the registry |
| `'can:'.InventoryPermission::TransfersReconcile->value` | `Concat` of a `String_` and an enum-case `->value` — **evaluated and ACCEPTED**; the resolved key is checked against the registry |
| `implode(',', [Enum::A->value, Enum::B->value])`, or an array of such expressions inside `->middleware([...])` | **evaluated and ACCEPTED**, element by element, when every element is itself constant |

Resolution uses `nikic/php-parser`'s `ConstExprEvaluator` with an **enum-case fallback that reads the case's declared value from the module enum's own AST** — no autoloading, no reflection on a booted app, so the test stays a pure static walk:

~~~php
$evaluator = new ConstExprEvaluator(function (Expr $expr): string {
    // PropertyFetch on a ClassConstFetch: Enum::Case->value
    if ($expr instanceof Expr\PropertyFetch
        && $expr->name instanceof Node\Identifier && $expr->name->toString() === 'value'
        && $expr->var instanceof Expr\ClassConstFetch
        && $expr->var->class instanceof Node\Name
        && $expr->var->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
        return $this->enumCaseValueFromAst($expr->var->class->toString(), $expr->var->name->toString());
    }

    throw new ConstExprEvaluationException('not statically resolvable');
});
~~~
`enumCaseValueFromAst()` maps the class name to `app/Modules/*/Domain/Enums/<short>.php` through a one-pass index built from the same glob, parses it once per test run, and returns the case's `String_` value.

**Only a genuinely non-constant expression is reported** — a key built from a variable, a function call, a match arm on request data — with: *"route permission key at `<file>:<line>` is not statically resolvable; a route gate must be a constant expression so CI can verify it exists."* **There are zero such sites in the tree today**, which the test's own first run proves.

It is an **AST walk, not a grep**, so nothing is silently skipped: every extracted argument is either resolved and checked, or reported as non-constant.

### 16c — `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization`

The eight live role-name authorization sites — `CreateDraftCountingRequest.php:35`, `InventoryCountingController.php:782,866,905,975,1297,1494`, `DiscountPermissionResolver.php:39` — **bypass token narrowing** and are a **wave 2a** conversion and a **wave 2b entry condition**. Wave 1 ships the **rule and its shrink-only baseline** so no ninth site can be added; it does **not** convert the eight. The rule matches `->hasRole(`, `->hasAnyRole(`, `->roles->contains(`, and `in_array($…->name, [ … ], true)` where the array elements are known role names.

> Spec §8's wave-1 deliverable list includes this rule and its ESLint companion. It is included here for that reason; the wave-1 brief's shorter list is not a scope reduction.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1** — write both PHPStan rules, register them in `apps/api/phpstan.neon` — `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` under `rules:` (`dev:…:33-41`, which takes no-argument rules) and `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` under `services:` (`dev:…:43-47`, which is where argument-taking rules already live, beside `ForbidFloatCastOnDecimalProperty`).
- [ ] **Step 2 — generate both shrink-only baselines** with the command above, and record the measured counts.
- [ ] **Step 3 — fixture pairs under `apps/api/tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/`** plus `ForbidPermissionStringLiteralTest` (the shape of `ForbidFixedScaleQuantityLiteralRuleTest`), proving the rule fires on **both** error classes and does **not** over-fire on config keys, i18n keys or a dotted string in a non-authorization argument. The same fixture pattern covers `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization`.
- [ ] **Step 4 — `RoutePermissionLiteralTest`** with all three accepted expression shapes and the one rejected one, each as a named case, plus `every_route_key_is_in_active_keys` over the real tree.
- [ ] **Step 5 — the ESLint companion** for the role-name idiom in `apps/web` (`no-role-name-authorization`), with its own shrink-only allowlist in `apps/web/eslint.config.js`.
- [ ] **Step 6 — run PHPStan over the whole `app/` and confirm the baseline absorbs exactly the pre-existing sites and no more.** A baseline that absorbs a site introduced by *this wave* is a bug: check the diff.
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
      ./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app

      cd ../web && pnpm lint
      ```

- [ ] **Step 7 — stage and commit:**

## Task 1.17: the generated frontend map and the label skeleton

**`permissions:export-frontend-map` keeps its signature, its output path, its sha256 header and the preflight drift guard.** W-LOT-A-1a's determinism gate asserts byte identity across two generations **and** the committed artifact, and **that gate must keep passing**. Only its **input** changes, from the seeder's two statics to `PermissionRegistry` — which, through Task 1.14's shims, is already what the statics return, so this task's diff is the constructor and two call sites:

~~~php
// ExportFrontendPermissionsMap — dev:apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ExportFrontendPermissionsMap.php
public function __construct(
    private readonly Filesystem $files,
    private readonly PermissionRegistry $registry,     // NEW
) {
    parent::__construct();
}

// in handle(), replacing ExportFrontendPermissionsMap.php:29-30
$contents = $this->render(
    $this->registry->keys(),
    $this->registry->templateGrants(),
    $this->registry->byModule(),                       // NEW — drives PERMISSION_MODULES
);
~~~
and the header's `// Source:` line moves from `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php` to `apps/api/app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/PermissionRegistry.php` (an intentional, one-line change to a hashed body — **the sha256 is computed over the body below the header**, so the drift guard is unaffected; verify that with the two-generation run rather than assuming it).

It gains one output and **one fixture the PHPStan rule consumes** (Task 1.16):

~~~ts
// apps/web/src/hooks/permissionsMap.generated.ts (appended, generated)
export const PERMISSION_MODULES = { 'inventory.transfers.view': 'Inventory', /* one entry per key */ } as const
export type Permission = keyof typeof PERMISSIONS
~~~
```
apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json   # sorted registry->keys(), written by the same command
```

`Permission` already exists as `keyof typeof PERMISSIONS`; what changes is that **wave 2a** stops widening it with `UiAliasPermission`. Wave 1 does not touch `usePermissions.ts`.

**The label gap is real and large.** `apps/web/src/locales/{en,fr,ar}/common.json` carry 40 module keys and 35 action keys, identical in all three locales, against 325 permissions — so the UI silently degrades to the raw key because `translateModule`/`translateAction` pass the raw string as the i18next fallback. **Re-measure the two gap numbers against the 38 modules and the actual action set** rather than carrying rev 1's `119 / 125`, which were computed against 304 keys and the old prefix grouping; record the measured pair in the handback.

`PermissionRegistryLabelCoverageTest` asserts that for every active key, `permissions.modules.<module>` and `permissions.actions.<action>` exist in **each** of the three files. **The test does not merely fail:** `php artisan permissions:export-label-skeleton` writes, per locale, a `common.permissions` block containing every missing key with the raw action as the value, so a translator receives a **complete file to edit** rather than a diff to reconstruct. The skeleton is **not committed as translations** — the command writes to `storage/app/permission-labels/<locale>.json` and the test's failure message points at it.

**English may be seeded from the skeleton mechanically. French and Arabic are human work, and are the reason this is a wave-1 deliverable with a named owner, not a side effect.**

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1** — re-point the exporter's input at the registry; add `PERMISSION_MODULES` and the keys fixture; **confirm the sha256 header and the preflight drift guard still behave**:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api && php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map && php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map && git diff --stat -- ../web/src/hooks/permissionsMap.generated.ts
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1 && ./scripts/preflight.sh --only=permissions-map 2>/dev/null || sed -n '136,149p' scripts/preflight.sh
      ```

      **Expected:** the second generation changes nothing.

- [ ] **Step 3 — close the English gap mechanically; open the French and Arabic work with a named owner.** If the translations are not available inside this lane, ship English in all three **and record it as residual R-w1-2** — but do **not** leave a key missing, because a missing key renders as the raw permission string in the operator's Roles matrix.
- [ ] **Step 4 — run, analyse, format, stage, commit:**

## Task 1.18: `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` over a versioned YAML runbook — **exact order, not presence**

**Gate r9's minor m-3, applied; plan gate r1's B1-5 and M1-5, applied on top.** The invariant is correct and stays; **parsing prose is brittle** — wording or Markdown layout can break the build without changing deployment, while semantically equivalent prose can evade a simplistic parser. The authoritative sequence therefore lives in a small **versioned machine-readable runbook**, and both the test and the human-facing documentation consume or validate **that** source.

**Two rev-2 corrections:**

1. **The YAML now carries the dry run**, as step 2 of every catalogue-changing wave from 1 onward, **after** `tenants:migrate-rolling`. Rev 1's YAML omitted it while rev 1's human Deploy table put it **first, before the migration** — and the first dry run would then have failed on missing `template_key` / `template_version` / `customised_at` / `is_system` columns (BLOCKER B1-5).
2. **The test asserts EXACT ORDER, not presence** (MAJOR M1-5). Rev 1's step 2 said only that each named command should "appear somewhere in the YAML", under which rev 1's own dry-run-before-migration row would have **passed**. The comparison is now `assertSame($yamlSteps, $documentSteps)` per wave — same commands, same order, same count.
3. **The human table is GENERATED from the YAML**, not written beside it. `php artisan permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=1` prints the Markdown rows, and the test asserts the plan's and the spec's rows are **byte-identical** to that output after whitespace normalisation. A prose row that disagrees fails with the wave name, the expected row and the found row.

`apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml`:

~~~yaml
# THE AUTHORITY for every wave's permission deploy sequence. The wave rows in
# docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md §8, in
# this wave's plan, and in the deploy runbook are VALIDATED AGAINST this file and
# GENERATED from it; this file is not generated from them.
#
# Rules PermissionDeploySequenceTest enforces:
#   (a) no fleet-scoped deploy step invokes `permissions:sync` — the per-tenant
#       command may appear ONLY under `remediation:`, with an explicit --tenants=
#       or --tenant= argument, never as a wave's deploy step;
#   (b) no deploy step invokes `tenants:run permissions:sync`,
#       `tenants:run permissions:ensure` or `tenants:seed` in ANY option shape;
#   (c) every wave from 1 onward whose catalogue_changes is true lists
#       `permissions:sync-fleet` AFTER `tenants:migrate-rolling` and BEFORE
#       `permission:cache-reset`, and lists the `--dry-run` invocation AFTER
#       `tenants:migrate-rolling` too — sync reads and writes the roles template
#       columns, so a dry run before the migration fails on missing columns;
#   (d) the human-facing tables in the spec and in each wave plan are BYTE-EQUAL
#       to `permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=<w>` after whitespace
#       normalisation — EXACT ORDER, not command presence.
version: 2
waves:
  - wave: "0b"
    catalogue_changes: true
    # ensure-fleet is allow-listed for 0b ONLY; wave 1 deletes it.
    steps:
      - "bash scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh"
      - "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
      - "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
    remediation:
      - "php artisan permissions:ensure-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug> --keys=<keys> --grant-to=<role>"
  - wave: "1"
    catalogue_changes: true
    steps:
      - "php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling"
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run"
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet"
      - "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
      - "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
    remediation:
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug>"
  - wave: "2a"
    catalogue_changes: false
    steps:
      - "php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling"
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run"
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet"
      - "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
      - "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
    remediation:
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug>"
  - wave: "2b"
    catalogue_changes: true
    steps:
      - "php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling"
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run"
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet"
      - "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
      - "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
    remediation:
      - "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug>"
~~~

> **The 0b row changed shape in rev 2, and that is not cosmetic.** Wave 0b rev 2's BLOCKER B0b-6 replaced its two bare `permissions:ensure-fleet` invocations with **one shell wrapper**, `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`, which is the only writer of the ensure status file. The runbook records the wrapper, so rule (b) is checkable and rev 1's trailing space after the ellipsis (`--keys=… `) — which would have broken byte-exact comparison — is gone with the row that carried it.

> **Why this guard matters more than the one-word correction that produced it.** Rev 8 of the spec's wave-2 row said `permissions:sync` — a command whose signature takes **one** `--tenant` or otherwise operates on the already-current tenant context, i.e. **no** tenant context at all in an entrypoint or a deploy shell. Wave 2 adds six manifest permissions **and** a fleet-wide `users` migration, so one context-free `permissions:sync` would have synchronised **zero** tenants — or exactly one, if a stray context existed — while the deploy reported success and every other tenant's `admin` silently lacked the new keys. **This is the second round in which a deploy row named the wrong entry point, and the third in which the plan's own prose disagreed with its machine-readable twin**, which is why the guard, not the correction, is the deliverable.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1** — write the YAML and `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` enforcing rules (a)–(d) over it. `permissions:ensure-fleet` is allow-listed **for wave 0b only**, and the allow-list is a constant naming the wave, not a regex.
- [ ] **Step 2 — `permissions:render-deploy-table`**, a tiny console command that reads the YAML and prints one Markdown table per wave, with the columns `# | Step | Notes` and the notes taken from an optional `note:` key per step. The plan's and the spec's Deploy tables are **replaced by its output**; the test regenerates and compares.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the exact-order assertion.** Per wave:
      ```php
      $this->assertSame(
          $yaml['waves'][$i]['steps'],
          $this->parseStepsFromMarkdown($documentPath, $wave),
          "wave {$wave}: the prose deploy sequence must match the runbook EXACTLY — same commands, same order.",
      );
      ```
      Presence-only comparison is what let rev 1's dry-run-before-migration row pass; `assertSame` over the ordered list is the fix.
- [ ] **Step 4 — three reds, each pasted into the handback:**
      1. add `php artisan permissions:sync` to wave 2a's YAML `steps` — the test must fail naming the wave and the command (rule a);
      2. **swap** wave 1's first two steps so the dry run precedes the migration — the test must fail naming wave 1 and the ordering rule (rule c). *This is the specific red that rev 1 could not produce.*
      3. edit one word of the plan's rendered Deploy table — the test must fail naming the wave, the expected row and the found row (rule d).
      Restore after each.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run, format, stage, commit:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
      php artisan test tests/Architecture/PermissionDeploySequenceTest.php
      ./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app/Console/Commands/RenderPermissionDeployTable.php
      ./vendor/bin/pint --test app/Console/Commands/RenderPermissionDeployTable.php tests/Architecture/PermissionDeploySequenceTest.php
      git add apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/PermissionDeploySequenceTest.php \
              apps/api/app/Console/Commands/RenderPermissionDeployTable.php \
              docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md
      ```
      Subject: **`Phase 1.18: add PermissionDeploySequenceTest over a versioned YAML runbook, with exact-order validation of the human tables`**, both trailers.

## Task 1.19: parity teardown, ceilings, preflight, soak and handback

- [ ] **Step 1 — delete `PermissionCatalogueParityTest` and its fixture.** It is **transitional by design** — it existed only to make the cut-over reviewable, and keeping it would freeze the catalogue against a snapshot the registry is now allowed to grow past. `git rm apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionCatalogueParityTest.php apps/api/tests/Fixtures/Authorization/permission-catalogue-parity.json` **in this commit**, and say so in the message. (`permission-catalogue-unassigned.json` and the progress test were already deleted in `Phase 1.4i`.)
      > **`PermissionRegistryCoverageTest` must be re-pointed off the parity fixture in the same commit**, or deleting the fixture reddens it. Its ownership map becomes the module→prefix constant it already embeds, read against `registry->byModule()` instead of against the frozen list.
- [ ] **Step 2 — run every test this wave owns, by path, on both lanes.** Fast lane first (registry, definition, verb, enums, SoD, scaffold, static guards, deploy sequence, label coverage), then the PG lane (sync, adoption, marker, fleet, race, migration, audit). **Never the full suite.** The exact two invocations are the Verification checklist's first two blocks.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the route-coverage ceilings.** Wave 1 gates **one** new route — `POST roles/{id}/reapply-template`, shipped **with** `can:roles.manage` — so both **uncovered** ceilings should be **unchanged** from wave 0b's `127 / 126`. Regenerate and confirm. **A change here is a finding**: it means either a manifest edit altered a route's classification, which it must not, or the new route landed ungated, which the anti-growth direction forbids.
- [ ] **Step 4 — manifest recompute, never a textual merge.** Recompute `gated_ceiling` and every touched group's `classes` against `dev`'s **current** values, with a `raise_note_2026_09_10` per changed group naming the classes and why they exist. **Count the files; do not trust an estimate written before the lane existed.** The measured pre-lane values are in Phase 0.
- [ ] **Step 5 — PHPStan L8, Pint, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `actionlint`** over every touched path.
- [ ] **Step 6 — the UI happy path, in the real app, with network and console capture.** Settings → **Roles** renders **grouped by registry module** (verify `catalog.attributes.*` now appears under **Catalog** and `inventory.transfers.*` under **Inventory** — the ★ rows of Task 1.4's table are the visible proof the grouping moved from the key prefix to the declared module) → every column header shows a **translated** action label, not a raw key → a system role shows the **template-version banner** → *Re-apply template* on a **pristine** system role is a visible **no-op** (empty diff, no toast claiming a change) → *Re-apply template* on a **customised** role restores the template set and clears the banner → editing a system role's permissions sets the *"customised on <date>"* state. **Zero 5xx, zero new console errors.**
- [ ] **Step 7 — the deploy dry run against the local demo fleet**, before any staging action, **and after the migration**:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
      php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling --force
      php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run ; echo "exit=$?"
      ```
      **Expected:** `exit=0`, one `PERMISSIONS-SYNC … mode=DRY_RUN` line per tenant with the full counter block, a closing `PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET` line, and **zero rows written** (verify with row counts on `permissions`, `roles` and `role_has_permissions` before and after). Then run it **for real** on the demo fleet and run it **again**, and paste all three markers: the second real run must read `outcome=ALREADY_CURRENT` with every `mutations` counter zero.
- [ ] **Step 8 — `tenant:census-day-one` clean.**
- [ ] **Step 9 — THE STAGING SOAK: one week with `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT=true`**, comparing each tenant's role-permission snapshot **before and after every deploy** and requiring **zero unintended diffs**. This is the direct replacement for the behaviour that caused I-20 — a destructive full reseed on every push — so it gets the evidence bar that lane established. **The soak is an exit condition of this wave, not a follow-up**: wave 2a's entry condition is *"wave 1 merged **and soaked**"*, and O-6/OQ-4 is answerable only from the soak's evidence.
- [ ] **Step 10 — write the handback** at `docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w1-<ISO date>.md`, containing:
      - the lane branch, its base SHA, and each task's commit SHA;
      - the **Phase-0 re-measurement** of `dev`, `lane/w-lot-a-1a` and `lane/t2-receipt-spine` beside this plan's `630afa86f` / `52f5ad796` / `951a7637e`;
      - the **Phase-0 signature verification** of the six inherited 0b artefacts, verbatim, including `fleetCommands()` and the three-way lock-coverage split;
      - the parity fixture's generated key count and its reconciliation (**expected 325**, Q-w1-2);
      - the **red evidence** for every task — at minimum: `Phase 1.4`'s partition red, `Phase 1.8`'s second-apply-writes-nothing red (the B1-3 red), EC-9c's customised `manager`, the marker negatives, the missing-admin fail-closed, the SoD hard half, **all three** deploy-sequence reds including the ordering one, EC-7's rename collision, and EC-20a's operator-versus-sync interleaving;
      - the seven `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` reference verdicts, one line each, **each with the command that produced it**;
      - the `permissions:report-dead` output beside the 22-key set, with any difference raised as a finding;
      - the SoD baseline (eighteen or the ruled-down number) and which O-5 answer it reflects;
      - the **measured** PHPStan literal-baseline counts (route-middleware strings; call sites) and the **measured** label-gap pair, both as numbers this lane produced rather than numbers this plan remembered;
      - the `DateFactory`-for-`Clock` substitution, named as a deliberate deviation from the spec's signature;
      - all three `permissions:sync-fleet` markers from step 7, and the ratchet ceilings before and after;
      - the UI probe's network and console output;
      - the manifest recompute with exact per-group deltas;
      - **the soak evidence**: per-tenant snapshots before and after each staging deploy for a week, and the count of unintended diffs (**required: zero**);
      - the six decisions and open questions, restated, with O-5 and O-6 marked **owner-owed**;
      - the residuals below;
      - a merge-readiness block: `git rev-parse --short dev`, `git merge-tree --write-tree dev <lane tip>` exit status, `git diff dev...<lane tip> --stat`, and `git diff <merge-base>..dev -- <every lane path>` (expected empty).
- [ ] **Step 11 — stage and commit:**
      ```
      git rm apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionCatalogueParityTest.php \
             apps/api/tests/Fixtures/Authorization/permission-catalogue-parity.json
      git add apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionRegistryCoverageTest.php \
      ```

      Subject: **`Phase 1.19: retire the transitional parity fixture, recompute the ceilings and hand back`**, both trailers. (The handback is an orchestrator document; its own commit keeps `docs(handoff): …` if it is split out, exactly as wave 0b's did.)

## Reviewer gate

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on the whole diff.** Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w1` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9.1
> §§4.2, 4.3.0a, 4.3.1–4.3.5, 4.5, 4.6.5, 4.7, 4.9, 5.2, 6 and 8's wave-1 row, and
> against this plan. Cite `file:line` for every claim; verify against the tree,
> never from the diff alone.
>
> 1. **Does the registry carry exactly the old catalogue, and is it 325?** Independently
>    derive the key set from the deleted seeder array (read it from `dev`), add the
>    lane's two wrapper keys and wave 0b's nineteen, apply the rename map, and compare
>    to `PermissionRegistry::keys()`. Name every key that moved, was added or was lost,
>    and say which is intentional. **If your count is 323 you read the wrong method:
>    `dev`'s `permissionNames()` is the lane's `legacyPermissionNames()`.**
> 2. **Was every commit green?** Walk `Phase 1.4a` … `Phase 1.4i` and confirm the
>    unassigned fixture shrinks monotonically to zero and that no commit ships a test
>    whose oracle arrives later.
> 3. **Is adoption really before every grant write?** Read `sync()` top to bottom
>    and list the steps in order. Then construct the tenant state under which a
>    customised role would be adopted pristine, and show why it cannot occur.
> 4. **Is the exact diff safe, and is the rerun genuinely zero-write?** For each of
>    the three role kinds, name the writes step 6 performs and prove a **custom** role
>    can lose nothing. Then find the `template_version` write and confirm it is
>    conditional, and find the query-log assertion that proves the second run writes
>    no row. A test asserting "no exception" is not that claim.
> 5. **Is `provisioning_source` written in exactly one case?** Find every write to
>    that column in the diff. There must be one, on a `general_manager` sync
>    creates in the same statement, on a tenant with no marker. Verify the lane's
>    CHECK is satisfied by construction on that path.
> 6. **Are there exactly two triggers?** Grep for `DatabaseMigrated`, for every
>    caller of `PermissionSyncService::sync()`, and for anything in a listener or
>    provider that could reach it. Then read the entrypoint and say what a deploy
>    does with the flag `false`.
> 7. **Did the stopgap go in ONE commit?** Confirm all eight artefacts, the three
>    superseded tests and the one rename are in the same commit as
>    `PermissionSyncService`, and that `SyncDryRunRollback` lands there too — there
>    must be no commit in which the dry-run path has no sentinel.
> 8. **Does the lock still make one domain?** Confirm wave 1 did not redefine the
>    key, the hash function or the row order; that the three new writers are in
>    `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`; that `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo` is in
>    **`LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`** with both entry points named, not in `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`;
>    and that `INITIALIZATION_ONLY` did not grow. Then check `RoleController::update`
>    writes `customised_at` in the **same** transaction as its `syncPermissions()`.
> 9. **Is the marker's counter split honest?** Name the ten mutations and the two
>    gauges, and confirm no gauge is asserted zero on a re-run anywhere.
> 10. **Dry run:** prove a `BLOCKED` dry run exits non-zero, on the per-tenant
>     command and on the fleet, and that a missing `admin` exits 1 on both paths.
> 11. **Compatibility mode:** confirm steps 1–4 run, step 5 does **not**, and the
>     `--tenant` refusal is exit 2.
> 12. **The static guards:** confirm the PHPStan baseline absorbs only pre-existing
>     sites, that the rule reads its key list from a generated file rather than
>     booting the app, and that `RoutePermissionLiteralTest` **accepts** the
>     `'can:'.Enum::Case->value` form — a guard that rejects the migration target
>     is a wall.
> 13. **Deploy order:** confirm the YAML, this plan's Deploy table and the spec's
>     wave-1 row all put `tenants:migrate-rolling` **before** every sync invocation
>     including the dry run, and that `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` compares
>     **ordered lists**, not command presence.
> 14. **SoD:** re-derive the baseline from the registry and compare to the
>     eighteen-row table. Confirm `purchase-orders.approve` is **not** declared
>     anywhere. Say whether O-5 was answered and, if not, whether the ceiling is
>     the right one to ship.
> 15. **Audit:** confirm the `AuditEvent` guard change does not alter the existing
>     company path, that `$this->companyId = $companyId ?? ''` is present (the
>     property is non-nullable), that `RoleSyncedV1` is registered in the subscriber,
>     and that a row actually lands.
> 16. **Overlap and the seeder:** confirm no other lane edits
>     `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, that the seven references are each disposed of
>     by an executed command rather than by inspection, and that
>     `RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest` is green and unedited.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory**, for the generated map and the label bundles. Ask specifically: is `permissionsMap.generated.ts` byte-identical across two generations **and** against the committed artifact (W-LOT's determinism gate)? Is `PERMISSION_MODULES` typed `as const` and consumed nowhere as a decision input (it is grouping data, not authorization)? Are all three locales complete, and is any Arabic or French value an English placeholder — and if so, is it recorded as a residual rather than shipped as done? Did the wave touch `usePermissions.ts` or `uiAliasPermissions.ts`? It must **not** — those are wave 2a.

**`fiscal-pos-reviewer` — advisory**, for the fourteen `pos.*` / `pos_orders.*` keys entering the deprecation set. Deprecation does not change runtime behaviour (the row survives one release), but the POS reads its approval scopes from `pos.approve_*` and a mis-deprecated key would remove a manager override. Ask: is every deprecated `pos.*` key genuinely unreachable from `apps/pos/src`, and does `permissions:report-dead`'s permission-shaped rule correctly exclude the `t('reports.view')` i18n hit?

No inventory, treasury or imports reviewer: wave 1 declares those modules' permissions but changes no inventory, treasury or import behaviour. **If a manifest's `templateDefaults` differs from the post-0b grant map for any Inventory or Treasury key, that is a behaviour change and the owning reviewer becomes mandatory** — say so in the handback rather than deciding it silently.

## Handback

`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w1-<ISO date>.md` — contents enumerated in Task 1.19 step 10.

### Residuals to record in it

| # | Residual | Owner / wave |
|---|---|---|
| **R-w1-1** | The eight **role-name** authorization sites (`CreateDraftCountingRequest.php:35`, `InventoryCountingController.php:782,866,905,975,1297,1494`, `DiscountPermissionResolver.php:39`) still bypass token narrowing. Wave 1 ships `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` and its shrink-only baseline so no ninth can be added; it converts none. Conversion is **wave 2a** and is a hard **entry condition of wave 2b** — `service-accounts.issue-token` stays unrouted until `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` is green, because issuing a narrowing token while a role-name idiom can bypass the narrowing would ship the intersection guarantee as a claim rather than a property. | wave 2a / 2b |
| **R-w1-2** | Any French or Arabic label shipped as an English placeholder. `permissions:export-label-skeleton` produces the complete file; the translation is human work with a named owner. | translator |
| **R-w1-3** | The **PHPStan literal baseline is large**. The door is closed behind it (a new literal is an error on day one) and the list only shrinks. The exact counts are **measured in the lane**, not carried from a prior revision. **Wave 3 empties it.** | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-4** | `LotActionPermissionDelta` survives as **dead code**. Sync never called it, so retirement is a deletion with no re-pointing and no behavioural change — once every tenant carries the marker. Likewise `SystemRoleName` and `SystemRoleTemplate` remain two enums with one asserted-equal value. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-5** | The `settings` **bare-manage/CRUD grandfather** is the only entry in `MANAGE_CRUD_GRANDFATHERED`. Retiring it means deciding whether `settings.manage` subsumes `settings.update` or the two gate genuinely different surfaces — an **owner question**, not a wave-1 rename. The list is shrink-only. **The three *qualified*-manage resources (`users`, `vehicles`, `catalog-cart`) are NOT exceptions**: a qualified `manage` is a narrowing of one facet and the rule is stated on the bare verb. | owner / wave 3 |
| **R-w1-6** | `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` does not exist. Step 3 **reports** orphans and never deletes; the `orphans` gauge will be non-zero on any tenant carrying a leftover row, forever, and that is not a failure. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-7** | **`marketplace.admin`** gates seller administration inside the tenant API yet is a platform-scoped capability — and under `admin = activeKeys()` **every tenant admin now holds it**. It is unreachable today (the module sits behind a kill-switch defaulting to false), which is why the correct fix — re-gate those routes on `super_admin` and deprecate the key — is wave 3 + ticket `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-marketplace-admin-platform-scope.md`. Inventing a `platformScoped` flag for one dormant key would be worse than the problem. **Verify the kill-switch default is still `false` at merge**, and say so in the handback. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-8** | **O-5 / OQ-3**, if unanswered at merge: the SoD baseline ships at **eighteen** and the ceiling with it. The recommended retirement is three from `manager` (+ the mirror on `general_manager`). The ratchet guarantees the number can only fall, so a later ruling is a follow-up lane, not a rework. | **owner** |
| **R-w1-9** | **O-6 / OQ-4**: whether `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT` becomes production-default `true` after the soak. This wave ships it `false` and produces the soak evidence the decision needs. The conservative alternative costs one runbook line and is a real option; it is worse only because a step a human can forget is a step a human will forget. | **owner**, at the end of the soak |
| **R-w1-10** | The **5-minute `/auth/me` staleness window** (EC-17) is untouched: on cold load Zustand hydrates roles/permissions from `localStorage` before `/auth/me` resolves, and `AuthProvider`'s `staleTime` means an open tab can render a five-minute-old permission set. It is a **UX-only** exposure — every mutating request is still authorized server-side — and it is recorded, not silently accepted. | out of scope |
| **R-w1-11** | Step 7 (**deprecation replacement grants**) is a **provable no-op in this wave**: all 22 deprecations carry `replacedBy: null`. The branch is implemented and tested with a throw-away deprecation, but it has **never run against real data**. The first wave that deprecates a key *with* a successor should treat its first fleet run as the branch's real evidence. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-12** | `restore` and `override` are declared `PermissionVerb` cases with **zero** keys using them. `PermissionVerbTest` snapshots that fact so it stays visible; the first key that uses either deletes one line of the test. | — |

## Deploy

**Generated from `apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml` (wave `1`) by `php artisan permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=1`, and validated against it byte-for-byte by `PermissionDeploySequenceTest`.** Editing this table by hand fails the build.

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | `php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling` | the four `roles` columns, the two partial uniques, the three CHECKs. **First, always** — sync's adoption and delta logic reads and writes `template_key`, `template_version`, `customised_at` and `is_system`, so a dry run before this fails on missing columns. |
| 2 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` | the only supported way to preview a deploy's authorization delta. WRITE-free and **not** failure-free: a rename collision, a not-ready tenant or a missing `admin` makes it exit non-zero, and a non-zero dry run means the applying run must not be started. |
| 3 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet` | **never** `tenants:run permissions:sync`; **never** the per-tenant `permissions:sync` without `--tenant=`; **there is no `--force`**. All three prohibitions are enforced by `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` against the YAML runbook. |
| 4 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | the boot-time global flush stays as a belt |
| 5 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | the map's input is now the registry |

**Deploy-checklist row.** *After deploy, confirm `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` reports `permissions_sync.healthy = true` — the **authenticated** super-admin endpoint at `apps/api/routes/api.php:83`; the public `/v1/health` is unchanged and still reports only `{status, timestamp}`. If it reports false, grep the Dokploy container log for the `PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET` aggregate line, which names every blocked or failed tenant id with its reason, and re-run `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=…` for those tenants — **each selector resolving to exactly one tenant**, per the step-1a contract — before considering the deploy complete.*

**Staging soak: one week with `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT=true`**, comparing each tenant's role-permission snapshot before and after every deploy and requiring **zero** unintended diffs. This is the direct replacement for the behaviour that caused I-20, so it gets the evidence bar that lane established, and it is the input to **O-6/OQ-4**.

---

## Rollback

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 1.19 (parity teardown) | the transitional test returns | — |
| Task 1.18 (deploy sequence) | the runbook guard disappears and the Deploy table stops being generated | — |
| Task 1.17 (FE map + labels) | the map's input returns to the seeder statics | Task 1.14 first (the statics must still be the array, not the shim); **and Task 1.16**, whose PHPStan rule reads `permission-registry-keys.json`, which this task generates |
| Task 1.16 (static guards) | the two rules and their baselines disappear | — |
| Task 1.15 (`permissions:scaffold`) | the generator disappears | — |
| Task 1.14 (the shim) | **the seeder shim reverts to the array in the same revert commit** — a shim without a registry is a class calling a service that does not exist | Tasks 1.4 and 1.3 in the same revert |
| Task 1.13 (SoD) | the baseline and the rule disappear | — |
| Task 1.12 (`customised_at` writer) | the operator edit stops marking customisation | **Task 1.10 first** — sync would otherwise keep applying deltas to roles an operator has edited, which is I-20's shape |
| Task 1.11 (triggers) | the deploy trigger and the health check disappear | the entrypoint's **previous** block must be restored **in the same revert commit**, or a deploy runs no catalogue step at all |
| **Task 1.10 (sync + commands + the stopgap deletion)** | no sync exists **and no `permissions:ensure` exists either** — reverting this commit restores all eight 0b artefacts along with the deletion, because they are one commit. That is the point of the atomicity: there is no intermediate state in which neither writer exists | Tasks 1.12 and 1.11 first |
| Task 1.9 (`RoleSyncedV1`) | sync's writes stop being audited | Task 1.10's steps 6 and 7 emit it — revert Task 1.10 or accept an unaudited replacement grant |
| Task 1.8 (applier + marker repo) | — | Task 1.10 first |
| **Task 1.7 (the `roles` migration)** | **`down()` REFUSES while any row has `template_key IS NOT NULL`** — so a rollback after a sync is a deliberate act that first requires clearing adoption, and never silently discards which roles were system roles | clear adoption first, deliberately |
| Task 1.6 (report-dead) | the scanner disappears; the `deprecate()` wraps stay (they live in Task 1.4's manifests) | — |
| Tasks 1.4–1.5 (manifests, enums) | the registry empties | Task 1.14 in the same revert |
| Tasks 1.1–1.3 (verb, definition, registry) | the catalogue machinery disappears | everything above first |

**`permissions:sync` is additive**, so a rollback leaves extra `permissions` rows on every tenant. They are **inert** once no route and no template references them, and `permissions:prune-orphans` is **not** run as part of a rollback — it does not exist until wave 3, and it is a deliberate operator action even then.

**The one thing a revert cannot undo** is a `template_key` already written by a sync that ran on a live tenant. That is what the migration's refusing `down()` is for: it makes clearing adoption an explicit, auditable act rather than a side effect of a `git revert`.

### Findings NOT applied, with the reason

| Gate ID | Why not |

| **M1-6**, the "author every test before its implementation" half | Applied as far as the document can carry it — every task's Step 1 is now *"write the test first"* and every red is named and reproducible — but the supplied class bodies are still printed **before** the test bodies in several tasks, because interleaving them at method granularity makes the plan unreadable as a dispatch document without changing what an executing agent produces.
